# History Form 4, Form Four History: authored answers for review

Every answer fixed by a person, not computed. Part 1: the tables, conventions and rules that computed answers come from.
Part 2: every authored question template, with all its data (keys, statements, pairs, choices and feedback) and sources.
Regenerate with `npm run review:f4-history`. Item IDs (TR-…) match `docs/teacher-review.md`.

## Part 1

Rules the computed answers use are listed in the subject’s library tests; every authored answer is in Part 2.

Compare that with the description asked for. | Expansionist policy involved deliberate conquest; economic hardship alone did not force that choice. |

### `y4-1-sequence` · Lesson 1 · ordering, level 2

Prompt: Put these dated events in chronological order, earliest first. Then use their sequence to distinguish an earlier condition from a later response.

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| a | b | c |
|---|---|---|

### `y4-1-assess` · Lesson 1 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the claim that misrepresents the history or the evidence.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ expansionism: a policy seeking territorial conquest and domination.
- ✅ appeasement: concessions intended to avoid war with an aggressive power.
- ✅ failure of collective security: the inability of international cooperation to restrain major aggression effectively.
- ✅ economic instability: hardship that increased political tensions without making aggression inevitable.
- ✅ immediate European trigger: Germany’s invasion of Poland leading to British and French declarations of war.
- ❌ Nazi expansion was simply a peaceful request with no conquest aim.  
  _↳ Expansionist policy involved deliberate conquest; economic hardship alone did not force that choice._
- ❌ The Munich settlement permanently ended German expansion.  
  _↳ The later occupation of Czech lands and invasion of Poland show that Munich did not prevent further aggression._
- ❌ The League successfully prevented every invasion in the interwar period.  
  _↳ Its ineffective response to major aggression weakened confidence in collective security._
- ❌ Every country experiencing depression necessarily chose aggressive war.  
  _↳ Economic crisis created pressures; political choices and different national circumstances still mattered._
- ❌ The invasion of Poland happened after Britain’s September declaration of war.  
  _↳ The invasion preceded those declarations; distinguish the immediate sequence from deeper causes._

### `y4-2-assess` · Lesson 2 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the claim that misrepresents the history or the evidence.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ colonial mobilisation: administrative recruitment of people and resources through imperial rule.
- ✅ Free French rallying: the shift of French-administered Cameroon to de Gaulle’s wartime movement.
- ✅ military contribution: service in armed units and their operational support.
- ✅ civilian and material contribution: work and supplies supporting operations beyond direct combat.
- ❌ Cameroon acted as a single independent government in the war.  
  _↳ The territories were under different colonial administrations; mobilisation reflected those structures._
- ❌ The Douala operation made both administered Cameroons independent.  
  _↳ Rallying changed wartime allegiance in French-administered Cameroon, not the territory’s independence status._
- ❌ Every African recruit served in exactly the same overseas battle.  
  _↳ Specific service must be established from a unit or individual record; origin alone does not identify a campaign._

### `y4-7-assess` · Lesson 7 · spot the error, level 3

### `y4-9-assess` · Lesson 9 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the claim that misrepresents the history or the evidence.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ self-determination: a people’s claim to decide its political future.
- ✅ nationalist mobilisation: organisation around collective political rights and self-government.
- ✅ Pan-African solidarity: cooperation among Africans and the diaspora across territorial boundaries.
- ✅ limited reform demand: a request for a particular improvement without necessarily demanding independent rule.
- ❌ Self-determination means permanent rule without the inhabitants’ wishes.

### `y4-10-assess` · Lesson 10 · spot the error, level 3
